Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying."

PFI2-016 · 第5次

保留邻块 · 目标要点 0.5

本文认为纸页抗张强度的提高主要来自PFI精磨过程中细纤维化和细组分的增加。 [1]
细纤维化和润胀使纤维在横向更柔顺，从而改善纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [1]
零距抗张指数通常用于表征纤维的拉伸强度。 [1]
零距抗张结果显示PFI精磨没有降低纤维强度，这与Kouko等的报道一致。 [1]
综合结果表明，PFI精磨提高了细纤维化、纤维润胀和纸页抗张强度，但没有出现纤维拉伸强度下降。 [1]

      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n20. Zhao, L.; Li, S.; Liang, C.; Qiao, L.; Du, K. High-strength and low-crystallinity cellulose/agarose composite microspheres: Fabrication, characterization and protein adsorption. Biochem. Eng. J. 2020, 107826. [CrossRef]\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n21. Wang, G.; Yang, X.; Wang, W. Reinforcing linear low-density polyethylene with surfactant-treated microfibrillated cellulose. Polymers 2019, 11, 441. [CrossRef] [PubMed]\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n22. Elazzouzi-Hafraoui, S.; Nishiyama, Y.; Putaux, J.; Heux, L.; Dubreuil, F.; Rochas, C. The shape and size distribution of crystalline nanoparticles prepared by acid hydrolysis of native cellulose. Biomacromolecules 2008, 9, 57–65. [CrossRef] [PubMed]",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nTable 2. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFigure 7. The deconvoluted FT-IR spectra of softwood fibers at various revolutions: (a) 0 r, (b) 4000 r, (c) 8000 r, (d) 12,000 r, and (e) 16,000 r.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFor the content of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the trend can be <sup>For</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>content</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>inter-molecular</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>softwood</sup> <sup>fiber,</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>trend</sup> <sup>can</sup> <sup>be</sup>divided into two stages, as shown in Table 2a. It decreased by 7.72% from 28.23% to 26.05% as refining from 0r to 4000 r and then increased by 9.14% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by a decrease of 17.13%, with the revolutions increasing from 8000 r to 16,000 r. Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying."
    ]
  }

删除邻块 · 目标要点 1

纸页抗张强度的提高主要归因于PFI精磨过程中的细纤维化和细组分（fines）。 [1]
精磨增加润胀（WRV）使纤维横向更柔顺，有助于提高纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [1]
零距抗张指数通常用于表征纤维本身的抗张强度。 [1]
零距抗张结果表明PFI精磨没有降低纤维强度，与Kouko等的报道一致。 [1]
综上，PFI精磨可在不降低纤维抗张强度的情况下提高纸页抗张强度；本文并未表明纸页抗张提高必然对应单根纤维强度提高。 [1]

Jev原判与全部维度

      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n21. Wang, G.; Yang, X.; Wang, W. Reinforcing linear low-density polyethylene with surfactant-treated microfibrillated cellulose. Polymers 2019, 11, 441. [CrossRef] [PubMed]",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",

Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFigure 7. The deconvoluted FT-IR spectra of softwood fibers at various revolutions: (a) 0 r, (b) 4000 r, (c) 8000 r, (d) 12,000 r, and (e) 16,000 r.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFor the content of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the trend can be <sup>For</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>content</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>inter-molecular</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>softwood</sup> <sup>fiber,</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>trend</sup> <sup>can</sup> <sup>be</sup>divided into two stages, as shown in Table 2a. It decreased by 7.72% from 28.23% to 26.05% as refining from 0r to 4000 r and then increased by 9.14% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by a decrease of 17.13%, with the revolutions increasing from 8000 r to 16,000 r. Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying."
    ]
  }
}

PFI2-028 · 第1次

保留邻块 · 目标要点 0

PFI精磨过程中纤维的纤丝化及性能与超分子结构的变化有关。 [1]
对针叶木纤维和阔叶木纤维，PFI精磨过程中CI-XI与O(6)H···O(3)’氢键含量呈显著正相关。 [1]
总体而言，PFI精磨过程中微晶尺寸、结晶度及各类氢键含量变化不大。 [1]
可认为PFI精磨是一种纤丝化效果良好、对超分子结构破坏相对较小的机械处理。 [1]

      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n23. Tribulová, T.; Kaˇcík, F.; Evtuguin, D.V.; Cabalov<sup>ˇ</sup> á, I.; Durkoviˇc, J. The e<sup>ˇ</sup> fects of transition metal sulfates on cellulose crystallinity during accelerated ageing of silver fir wood. Cellulose 2019, 26, 2625–2638.       "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n6. Li, Z.; Liu, W.; Guan, F.; Li, G.; Song, Z.; Yu, D.; Wang, H.; Liu, H. Using cellulose fibers to fabricate transparent paper by microfibrillation. 删除邻块 · 目标要点 1

所给证据仅包含 Li-2020 PFI 论文，未包含《Non-Wood Fibers: Relationships of Fiber Properties with Pulp Properties》的实验数据或结论，因此无法据此比较两篇论文的全部实验数据并给出共同结论。 [1]
PFI 论文指出，纤维的纤丝化及性能与 PFI 精磨过程中超分子结构的变化相关。 [1]
对软木纤维和硬木纤维，PFI 精磨过程中 CI-XI 与 O(6)H···O(3)’ 氢键含量呈显著正相关。 [1]
该研究认为，结晶尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量总体变化不大。 [1]
作者据此推测，PFI 精磨是一种对超分子结构破坏相对较小的优良机械纤丝化处理。 [1]

Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally speculated based on CI-XI while refining. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds did not change much in general.       "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nupramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.PFI refining acts not only on the macro-level structure and mechanical properties, but also on The results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of thethe supramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers.       "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n24. Kouko, J.; Jajcinovic, M.; Fischer, W.; Ketola, A.; Hirn, U.; Retulainen, E. Efect of mechanically induced micro deformations on extensibility and strength of individual softwood pulp fibers and sheets. Cellulose 2019, 26, 1995–2012. [CrossRef]",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n7. Zhao, D.; Yang, F.; Dai, Y.; Tao, F.; Shen, Y.; Duan, W.; Zhou, X.; Ma, H.; Tang, L.; Li, J. Exploring crystalline structural variations of cellulose during pulp beating of tobacco stems. Carbohydr. Polym. 2017, 174, 146–153. [CrossRef] [PubMed]",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n10. Gao, W.; Xiang, Z.; Chen, K.; Yang, R.; Yang, F. Efect of depth beating on the fiber properties and enzymatic saccharification eficiency of softwood kraft pulp. Carbohydr. Polym. 2015, 127, 400–406. [CrossRef]"

PFI2-028 · 第2次

保留邻块 · 目标要点 0

Li-2020 论文研究 PFI refining 对 softwood fibers 和 hardwood fibers 的细纤维化、润胀、力学性能及超分子结构的影响。 [1] [3] [4]
PFI refining 改善细纤维化（尤其内部细纤维化）、纤维润胀和纸页抗张强度，且不降低纤维本身的抗张强度。 [3]
PFI refining 不仅作用于宏观结构和力学性能，也作用于超分子结构，包括微晶尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量。 [1]
在 PFI refining 过程中，softwood fibers 和 hardwood fibers 的 CI-XI 与 O(6)H···O(3)’ 氢键含量呈显著正相关。 [1]
总体而言，PFI refining 过程中微晶尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量变化不大，可认为该处理对超分子结构破坏相对较小。 [1]

The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Conclusions\n[原文]\nupramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.PFI refining acts not only on the macro-level structure and mechanical properties, but also on The results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of thethe supramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI refining is an excellent mechanical treatment of fibrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4.       "ref": "[4]",
      "page_start": 1,
      "page_end": 2,
      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "verification": {
        "status": "needs_source_review",
        "exact_other_pages": [],
        "note": "A text extraction mismatch is not automatically a false citation or a zero quality score."
      }

      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nupramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.PFI refining acts not only on the macro-level structure and mechanical properties, but also on The results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of thethe supramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally speculated based on CI-XI while refining. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI refining is an excellent mechanical treatment of fibrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4.       "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n23. Tribulová, T.; Kaˇcík, F.; Evtuguin, D.V.; Cabalov<sup>ˇ</sup> á, I.; Durkoviˇc, J. The e<sup>ˇ</sup> fects of transition metal sulfates on cellulose crystallinity during accelerated ageing of silver fir wood. Cellulose 2019, 26, 2625–2638. 删除邻块 · 目标要点 1

在所提供的证据中，可核实的是 Li-2020 PFI 论文的结论；未提供《Non-Wood Fibers: Relationships of Fiber Properties with Pulp Properties》的实验数据或结论，因此不能对两篇论文的全部实验数据进行逐一比较或归纳共同结论。 [1] [3]
Li-2020 的结论指出，PFI refining 引起的细纤维化与纤维性质变化同超分子结构（晶粒尺寸、结晶度及各类氢键含量）的变化相关。 [1]
该文报告 CI-XI 与 O(6)H···O(3)’ 氢键含量在软木纤维和硬木纤维的 PFI refining 过程中显著正相关。 [1]
该文还报告晶粒尺寸、结晶度及各类氢键含量总体变化不大，并认为 PFI refining 是相对少破坏超分子结构的优秀机械细纤维化处理。 [1]
该文实验结果显示，随着 PFI refining 转数增加，打浆度和 WRV 显著提高，细纤维化、纤维润胀及片材抗张强度提高，且零-span 抗张指数表明纤维强度未降低。 [3]

{
  "task_id": "PFI2-028",
  "answer": "在所提供的证据中，可核实的是 Li-2020 PFI 论文的结论；未提供《Non-Wood Fibers: Relationships of Fiber Properties with Pulp Properties》的实验数据或结论，因此不能对两篇论文的全部实验数据进行逐一比较或归纳共同结论。 [1] [3]\nLi-2020 的结论指出，PFI refining 引起的细纤维化与纤维性质变化同超分子结构（晶粒尺寸、结晶度及各类氢键含量）的变化相关。 [1]\n该文报告 CI-XI 与 O(6)H···O(3)’ 氢键含量在软木纤维和硬木纤维的 PFI refining 过程中显著正相关。 [1]\n该文还报告晶粒尺寸、结晶度及各类氢键含量总体变化不大，并认为 PFI refining 是相对少破坏超分子结构的优秀机械细纤维化处理。 [1]\n该文实验结果显示，随着 PFI refining 转数增加，打浆度和 WRV 显著提高，细纤维化、纤维润胀及片材抗张强度提高，且零-span 抗张指数表明纤维强度未降低。 [3]",

      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nupramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.PFI refining acts not only on the macro-level structure and mechanical properties, but also on The results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of thethe supramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally speculated based on CI-XI while refining. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI refining is an excellent mechanical treatment of fibrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4.       "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n24. Kouko, J.; Jajcinovic, M.; Fischer, W.; Ketola, A.; Hirn, U.; Retulainen, E. Efect of mechanically induced micro deformations on extensibility and strength of individual softwood pulp fibers and sheets. Cellulose 2019, 26, 1995–2012. [CrossRef]",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n7. Zhao, D.; Yang, F.; Dai, Y.; Tao, F.; Shen, Y.; Duan, W.; Zhou, X.; Ma, H.; Tang, L.; Li, J. Exploring crystalline structural variations of cellulose during pulp beating of tobacco stems. Carbohydr. Polym. 2017, 174, 146–153. [CrossRef] [PubMed]",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n10. Gao, W.; Xiang, Z.; Chen, K.; Yang, R.; Yang, F. Efect of depth beating on the fiber properties and enzymatic saccharification eficiency of softwood kraft pulp. Carbohydr. Polym. 2015, 127, 400–406. [CrossRef]"
    ]
  }

PFI2-028 · 第3次

保留邻块 · 目标要点 1

提供的证据仅包含 Li-2020 的 PFI 精炼论文，未包含《Non-Wood Fibers: Relationships of Fiber Properties with Pulp Properties》的正文或实验数据，因此无法对两篇论文的全部实验数据进行逐项比较。 [1] [3] [4]
PFI 精炼提高了纤维的细纤维化程度（尤其是内部细纤维化）、纤维润湿性和纸页抗张强度，且未降低纤维本身的抗张强度。 [3]
打浆度和 WRV 随 PFI 精炼转数的增加显著提高，原因是更高程度的细纤维化产生了新的表面和更多孔隙。 [3]
抗张指数的提高主要归因于 PFI 精炼过程中的细纤维化和细小纤维的增加。 [3]
与阔叶木纤维相比，针叶木纤维的湿纸幅强度提高更显著，纤维长度对湿纸幅强度起重要作用。 [3]
零抗张指数结果表明，PFI 精炼没有降低纤维强度，这与 Kouko 等的报道一致。 [3]
纤维的细纤维化及其性能与 PFI 精炼过程中超分子结构（包括微晶尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量）的变化相关。 [1]
对针叶木和阔叶木纤维，PFI 精炼过程中 CI-XI 与 O(6)H···O(3)' 氢键含量呈显著正相关，因此可依据 CI-XI 推测该氢键含量的变化趋势。 [1]
总体而言，微晶尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量变化不大，PFI 精炼被认为是一种对超分子结构破坏相对较小的优良机械细纤维化处理。 [1]

  "answer": "提供的证据仅包含 Li-2020 的 PFI 精炼论文，未包含《Non-Wood Fibers: Relationships of Fiber Properties with Pulp Properties》的正文或实验数据，因此无法对两篇论文的全部实验数据进行逐项比较。 [1] [3] [4]\nPFI 精炼提高了纤维的细纤维化程度（尤其是内部细纤维化）、纤维润湿性和纸页抗张强度，且未降低纤维本身的抗张强度。 [3]\n打浆度和 WRV 随 PFI 精炼转数的增加显著提高，原因是更高程度的细纤维化产生了新的表面和更多孔隙。 [3]\n抗张指数的提高主要归因于 PFI 精炼过程中的细纤维化和细小纤维的增加。 [3]\n与阔叶木纤维相比，针叶木纤维的湿纸幅强度提高更显著，纤维长度对湿纸幅强度起重要作用。 [3]\n零抗张指数结果表明，PFI 精炼没有降低纤维强度，这与 Kouko 等的报道一致。 [3]\n纤维的细纤维化及其性能与 PFI 精炼过程中超分子结构（包括微晶尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量）的变化相关。 [1]\n对针叶木和阔叶木纤维，PFI 精炼过程中 CI-XI 与 O(6)H···O(3)' 氢键含量呈显著正相关，因此可依据 CI-XI 推测该氢键含量的变化趋势。 [1]\n总体而言，微晶尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量变化不大，PFI 精炼被认为是一种对超分子结构破坏相对较小的优良机械细纤维化处理。 [1]",

Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally speculated based on CI-XI while refining. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI refining is an excellent mechanical treatment of fibrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4.       "ref": "[4]",
      "page_start": 1,
      "page_end": 2,
      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",

      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nupramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.PFI refining acts not only on the macro-level structure and mechanical properties, but also on The results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of thethe supramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally speculated based on CI-XI while refining. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI refining is an excellent mechanical treatment of fibrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4.       "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] References\n[原文]\n23. Tribulová, T.; Kaˇcík, F.; Evtuguin, D.V.; Cabalov<sup>ˇ</sup> á, I.; Durkoviˇc, J. The e<sup>ˇ</sup> fects of transition metal sulfates on cellulose crystallinity during accelerated ageing of silver fir wood. Cellulose 2019, 26, 2625–2638. 删除邻块 · 目标要点 1

提供的证据仅涉及 Li-2020 的 PFI 精炼论文，未包含《Non-Wood Fibers: Relationships of Fiber Properties with Pulp Properties》的实验数据或结论。 [1] [3] [4]
因此无法对两篇论文的全部实验数据做逐项比较，也不能据此给出两篇的共同结论。 [1] [3] [4]
PFI 论文报告纤颤和纤维性能与 PFI 精炼过程中超分子结构变化有关，且 CI-XI 与 O(6)H···O(3)’ 氢键含量呈显著正相关。 [1]
PFI 论文指出结晶尺寸、结晶度和各类氢键含量总体变化不大，因而 PFI 精炼对超分子结构破坏相对较小。 [1]
PFI 论文结果显示 PFI 精炼提高纤颤、纤维润胀和纸页抗张强度，同时不降低纤维抗张强度。 [3]

Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally speculated based on CI-XI while refining. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI refining is an excellent mechanical treatment of fibrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4.       "ref": "[4]",
      "page_start": 1,
      "page_end": 2,
      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "verification": {
        "status": "needs_source_review",
        "exact_other_pages": [],
        "note": "A text extraction mismatch is not automatically a false citation or a zero quality score."
      }
    }

    "kind": "supplied_excerpts",
    "texts": [
      "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nupramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.PFI refining acts not only on the macro-level structure and mechanical properties, but also on The results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of thethe supramolecular structure, including crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nThe results indicated that the fibrillation and properties of fibers are related to the variation of the supramolecular structure during PFI refining. It is noticeable that there was a significantly positive correlation between the CI-XI and content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds during PFI refining for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers. Thus, the trend of the content of O(6)H· · · O(3)’ H-bonds might be generally speculated based on CI-XI while refining. Besides, the crystallite size, crystallinity, and content of various H-bonds did not change much in general. It is assumed that PFI refining is an excellent mechanical treatment of fibrillation with relatively little destruction on the supramolecular structure.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4. Conclusions\n[原文]\nSupplementary Materials: The following are available online at http://www.mdpi.com/2073-4360/12/12/2912/s1, Figure S1: XRD fitting results of softwood fibers: (a) 0, (b) 4000, (c) 8000, (d) 12,000, (e) 16,000 revolutions of PFI refining, Figure S2: XRD fitting results of hardwood fibers: (a) 0, (b) 4000, (c) 8000, (d) 12,000, (e) 16,000 revolutions of PFI refining, Figure S3: Nonlinear fitting curves of the band of hardwood fibers for hydroxyl groups: (a) 0, (b) 4000, (c) 8000, (d) 12,000, (e) 16,000 revolutions of PFI refining, Figure S4: The BET N2 adsorption/desorption isotherms at various refining revolutions of pulp fibers: (a) softwood; (b) hardwood, Table S1: Cumulative pore volume, average pore size and specific surface area of softwood pulp fiber through refining, Cumulative pore volume, average pore size and specific surface area of hardwood pulp fiber through refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 4.
